**Short answer: No — we have completed the core control-plane (roughly Phases 0–4, 7, 9, 10 partially, plus two unplanned repairs), but the majority of the operational/telemetry/qualification workstream (Phases 5–6, 8, 11–16) is still open.**

---

# MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager — Implementation Plan v0.2.1

**Status:** Design decisions incorporated; electricity-cost composition corrected; ready for read-only infrastructure preflight before deployment scripting  
**Decision date:** 2026-09-08  
**Target Proxmox host:** `MIAM-00135`  
**Candidate VMID:** `108` — **must be proven unused before creation**  
**Candidate IP:** `10.0.20.108` — **must be proven unused before assignment**  
**DNS name:** `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal`  
**Primary purpose:** Central control plane, OpenAI-compatible API gateway, Hermes-agent key authority, routing layer, benchmarking system, observability system, and cost/performance database for the MARION-IA-USA local LLM infrastructure.

---

## 1. Executive objective

Create a dedicated VM named:

```text
MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager
```

on `MIAM-00135`, initially bootstrapped from the known-good GPU Driver Installer controller VM architecture but fully re-identified and stripped of copied GDI secrets/state.

The LLM Manager will become the stable point through which local Hermes agents and other authorized internal clients use LLM inference. It should:

- monitor and control vLLM deployments on `MIAM-00111`, `MIAM-00112`, `MIAM-00143`, and `MIAM-00144`
- issue a distinct API key to each Hermes agent
- expose a stable OpenAI-compatible endpoint
- route requests to healthy local deployments first
- wait a deliberate, configurable interval for local capacity instead of prematurely buying cloud inference
- fall back to OpenRouter only when policy permits and local capacity/health criteria are not satisfied
- enforce OpenRouter spend caps and per-agent policy
- benchmark model/topology combinations and keep historical results
- collect GPU, vLLM, request, health, and power telemetry
- calculate energy cost and performance-per-dollar
- use GPU onboard telemetry as the primary dynamic local power signal
- ingest Emporia whole-circuit/PDU/mini-split data as an independent facility-level measurement source
- store operational metadata and history in PostgreSQL
- offload high-volume or full prompt/response history away from the constrained `MIAM-00135` system disk
- provide controlled vLLM service lifecycle actions and bounded VM recovery actions
- provide a future path for multi-node vLLM deployments using the 100 Gb interconnect between `MIAM-00111`, `MIAM-00143`, and `MIAM-00144`
- eventually compare/rank local inference, OpenRouter, direct APIs, and other officially supported provider integrations by availability, quality, cost, performance, context, privacy, and policy

The LLM Manager is a **control plane and gateway**. It does not normally host model weights or perform GPU inference itself.

---

## 2. Decisions locked in v0.2

The following answers from Section 28 of v0.1 are now treated as design decisions.

| Item | v0.2 decision |
|---|---|
| VM identity | Candidate `VMID 108`, candidate `10.0.20.108`; fail closed unless both are proven unused |
| Gateway | LiteLLM Proxy plus a Startup Teams custom management/control web application |
| Client keys | One distinct API key per Hermes agent |
| Qwen 3.6 normal load | Design around about 10 concurrent agents/requests, with an initial hard concurrency envelope of about 20 pending benchmark qualification |
| Routing default | Local first, deliberate wait/queue, OpenRouter fallback only if the key policy allows it |
| Cloud policy | Default OpenRouter master/aggregate budget must have hard caps; individual keys may override fallback permissions within administrator limits |
| VM control | Manager may control both vLLM service lifecycle and bounded inference-VM recovery/restart |
| Power | GPU telemetry first; modeled CPU/RAM/platform overhead; Emporia used as independent whole-circuit/facility telemetry |
| Electricity planning rate | Rate 400 base energy (`$0.09992/kWh` Sep-May; `$0.13398/kWh` Jun-Aug) + year-round Emporia-observed variable fees/riders adder of `$0.05830/kWh`; effective planning rates `$0.15822/kWh` Sep-May and `$0.19228/kWh` Jun-Aug (displayed as 15.82¢ and 19.23¢) |
| Logging | Metadata-first initially; architecture must support full prompt/response logs later with off-host storage |
| Admin auth | Existing LDAP/LLDAP identity system; local break-glass/bootstrap admin only if needed |
| DNS | `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal` |
| Database | PostgreSQL; application designed so PostgreSQL can reside on a future storage-oriented VM on `MIAM-00147` |
| Fallback timing | Local requests are given meaningful time to queue/run; slow local TTFT alone is not a reason to immediately duplicate the request in OpenRouter |
| Distributed inference | 100 Gb multi-node path is a first-class future capability but remains unroutable until independently qualified |

### Important interpretation: API keys versus concurrency

The number of issued API keys should **not** be limited to 20. API keys identify clients; concurrency consumes model capacity.

For the known `Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ` deployment, start with a deployment profile such as:

```yaml
recommended_concurrency: 10
initial_hard_concurrency_limit: 20
max_issued_agent_keys: unlimited_by_model_capacity
```

The concurrency values must be model/deployment specific. A different model, quantization, topology, context target, or GPU set may receive a different admission envelope.

---

## 3. Known inference baseline

### 3.1 MIAM-00111 qualified baseline

Known qualified environment:

```text
Host:             MIAM-00111
Proxmox:          VE 9.2.10
Inference VM:     VM103 jordatech-vllm-test
Guest:            Ubuntu 24.04.4 LTS
Kernel:           6.8.0-139-generic
GPUs:             6 x RTX 3080 20 GB
Aggregate VRAM:   120 GB
NVIDIA driver:    R580 open, module 580.173.02
Python env:       /opt/vllm-venv
API port:         8000
Model:            QuantTrio/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ
```

Working topology:

```text
TP1 / DP6 / EP6
```

Core flags:

```text
--tensor-parallel-size 1
--data-parallel-size 6
--enable-expert-parallel
--expert-placement-strategy linear
--quantization awq
--dtype half
--gpu-memory-utilization 0.92
--kv-cache-dtype turboquant_k8v4
--attention-backend TURBOQUANT
--enable-prefix-caching
--enable-chunked-prefill
--scheduler-reserve-full-isl
--watermark 0.05
--kv-cache-metrics
```

Validated large-context operating point:

```text
10 concurrent requests
229,376 input tokens per request
+ 256 output tokens per request
```

Do not record the synthetic 250k test as a qualified model limit because tokenizer expansion exceeded the declared 262,144-token model maximum.

Representative 4096-in/256-out throughput results:

| Concurrency | Output tok/s | Total tok/s | Mean TTFT |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 249.85 | 4259.49 | 4.27 s |
| 16 | 271.21 | 4623.65 | 4.73 s |
| 20 | 311.98 | 5318.69 | 5.20 s |
| 32 | 319.69 | 5450.14 | 7.39 s |
| 48 | 348.62 | 5943.41 | 11.13 s |

For normal Hermes service, concurrency 10 remains the initial preferred baseline even though aggregate throughput rises at higher concurrency.

### 3.2 MIAM-00112

Known major difference:

```text
6 x RTX 5060 Ti 16 GB
AMD Threadripper Pro 3955WX
128 GB RAM
```

Do not copy the MIAM-00111 topology without qualification. Its 96 GB aggregate VRAM and different GPU architecture require a separate model-fit and benchmark envelope.

### 3.3 MIAM-00143 and MIAM-00144

These are intended to reproduce or complement the MIAM-00111 inference stack, but each must independently inventory:

```text
GPU model/count/VRAM
PCI BDFs
IOMMU groups
kernel
NVIDIA driver/module packages
inference VM resources
100 Gb NICs/ports
network addresses
RDMA capability
GPU-to-NIC PCIe topology
```

Never copy PCI BDFs between hosts.

---

```

---

## 5. Base VM and bootstrap strategy

### 5.1 Candidate VM sizing

Start with:

```text
VMID:             108 candidate only
IP:               10.0.20.108 candidate only
Hostname:         marion-ia-usa-llm-manager
Display name:     MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager
OS:               Ubuntu Server 24.04 LTS
vCPU:             8
RAM:              16 GB
System disk:      64 GB
Local data disk:  64-128 GB only if actually available/needed
Machine:          q35
Firmware:         OVMF
QEMU agent:       enabled
Ballooning:       disabled
Autostart:        enabled after acceptance test
GPU passthrough:  none
```

Because `MIAM-00135` has constrained storage, do not assume a large local telemetry/database disk. Keep all high-volume paths configurable and relocatable.

### 5.2 Clone source

Use the existing controller:

```text
VM107
gpu-driver-installer-v4-1
10.0.20.107:8443
```

as a **bootstrap image only**.

Required clone hygiene:

1. Full-clone while source is in a consistent state.
2. Keep clone network-isolated until identity reset is complete.
3. Change VM display name and guest hostname.
4. Regenerate machine-id.
5. Regenerate SSH host keys.
6. Remove copied application secrets, private keys, audit records, state, tokens, and TLS identity.
7. Remove GDI application code/services not needed by LLM Manager.
8. Retain only useful base hardening, QEMU guest agent, service patterns, reverse-proxy/TLS patterns, and OS configuration.
9. Generate a new TLS certificate for `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal`.
10. Run a secret scan before enabling the new network interface.

### 5.3 Fail-closed VMID/IP preflight

Before any clone or network mutation, the deployment script must prove both identifiers are unused.

VMID validation should include at least:

```text
cluster-wide Proxmox resource lookup for VMID 108
MIAM-00135 qm list
/etc/pve configuration search
```

IP validation should include at least:

```text
existing Proxmox guest configuration search
DNS lookup
neighbor/ARP table lookup
ICMP probe
ARP duplicate-address detection from the correct VLAN
DHCP/static reservation inspection where available
```

A failed probe does **not** prove an IP is unused. Any contradictory or ambiguous signal aborts deployment.

---

## 6. Software stack

### 6.1 Gateway

Use **LiteLLM Proxy** as the provider-normalization and virtual-key gateway.

Reasons:

- OpenAI-compatible API surface
- virtual keys
- authentication/authorization hooks
- spend tracking and budgets
- rate limiting
- provider normalization
- retries/fallbacks
- local OpenAI-compatible/vLLM endpoints
- OpenRouter and future external provider adapters

The custom Startup Teams control application remains authoritative for infrastructure-specific logic such as vLLM lifecycle, GPU health, power, benchmark qualification, host maintenance, and MARION-specific routing eligibility.

### 6.2 Custom control application

Recommended:

```text
Python 3.12
FastAPI
Uvicorn/Gunicorn
SQLAlchemy
Alembic
PostgreSQL
systemd
Nginx or Caddy
```

Suggested layout:

```text
/opt/llm-manager/
    app/
    control/
    collectors/
    routing/
    benchmark/
    auth/
    power/
    providers/
    distributed/
    migrations/
    static/
    templates/

/etc/llm-manager/
    manager.yaml
    nodes.yaml
    models.yaml
    routes.yaml
    tls/
    secrets/

/var/lib/llm-manager/
    cache/
    benchmark-artifacts/
    exports/
    spool/

/var/log/llm-manager/
```

### 6.3 Service separation

Use separate systemd units where practical:

```text
llm-manager-web.service
llm-manager-collector.service
llm-manager-benchmark.service
llm-manager-control.service
litellm.service
```

Do not let a hung benchmark worker take down the gateway.

---

## 7. Network, DNS, TLS, and access

Expose one HTTPS name:

```text
https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/
```

Recommended routing:

```text
/admin      -> custom admin UI
/api        -> manager orchestration API
/v1         -> LiteLLM/OpenAI-compatible API gateway
/healthz    -> manager health
```

Access policy:

```text
LAN:             allowed
Tailscale:       allowed where policy permits
Public Internet: denied
```

Hermes clients should only require:

```bash
OPENAI_BASE_URL=https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/v1
OPENAI_API_KEY=<agent-specific-key>
OPENAI_MODEL=startupteams/general
```

They should never require:

- SSH access to inference servers
- Proxmox credentials
- vLLM host credentials
- OpenRouter master credentials
- direct provider master credentials

---

## 8. Admin authentication and authorization

The web UI should authenticate against the existing LDAP/LLDAP environment.

Recommended roles:

```text
LLM-Viewer
LLM-Operator
LLM-Admin
LLM-Security-Admin
```

Example permissions:

| Capability | Viewer | Operator | Admin | Security Admin |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| View status/metrics | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Run benchmark | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Drain/undrain | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Restart vLLM service | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Restart inference VM | No | Restricted | Yes | Yes |
| Edit deployment config | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| Create/rotate agent keys | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| Change provider master secrets | No | No | No | Yes |
| Change global spend caps | No | No | Yes | Yes |

A local bootstrap/break-glass administrator may exist for installation/recovery, but LDAP should be the routine administrative identity source.

**Secret handling:** do not hardcode or reproduce the root password, LDAP bind password, Emporia password, OpenRouter key, or LiteLLM master key in this plan, source control, shell history, or generated configuration. Inject them through root-readable secret files or a later secret manager.

---

## 9. Hermes API-key and policy model

### 9.1 One key per Hermes agent

Structure:

```text
Organization
  -> Project/service
      -> Hermes agent
          -> one or more rotating API credentials
```

Each stored key record should include:

```text
key_id
key_prefix
hash
agent_id
project_id
created_at
expires_at optional
enabled
allowed_logical_models
max_concurrency
requests_per_minute optional
tokens_per_minute optional
daily_token_budget optional
monthly_token_budget optional
daily_dollar_budget optional
monthly_dollar_budget optional
local_allowed
cloud_allowed
fallback_policy
provider_allowlist optional
logging_policy
```

Raw keys are shown only at creation/rotation.

### 9.2 Initial policy profiles

Support at least:

```text
LOCAL_ONLY
LOCAL_FIRST_OPENROUTER_FALLBACK
CLOUD_ALLOWED_EXPLICIT
SPECIFIC_MODEL_ONLY
COST_CAPPED
BENCHMARK_ONLY
```

Recommended default for normal Hermes agents:

```text
LOCAL_FIRST_OPENROUTER_FALLBACK
```

but only after OpenRouter global caps are configured.

### 9.3 OpenRouter budget controls

Before enabling fallback for any default/general key:

```text
[ ] global daily spend cap configured
[ ] global monthly spend cap configured
[ ] warning threshold configured
[ ] hard-stop threshold configured
[ ] per-project cap supported
[ ] per-agent cap supported
[ ] audit event generated on fallback
[ ] actual OpenRouter reported cost stored when available
```

Fallback should never silently convert an uncapped local workload into uncapped external spend.

---

## 10. Routing and admission policy

### 10.1 Default sequence

For a normal general-purpose Hermes request:

```text
1. Resolve logical model and agent policy.
2. Find healthy local deployments that satisfy context/capability constraints.
3. Apply deployment admission limits.
4. If local capacity is temporarily full, queue for the configured local patience interval.
5. If admitted locally, let the local request continue even if TTFT is slow.
6. Do not start a duplicate OpenRouter request merely because local TTFT is slower than expected.
7. If no local deployment becomes admissible before the queue deadline, and the key permits cloud fallback, use OpenRouter.
8. If local execution actually fails after dispatch and a retry is safe, apply the configured retry/fallback policy.
9. If cloud fallback is disallowed or budget-capped, return an explicit unavailable/capacity response.
```

### 10.2 Initial timeout model

Use separate timeouts for separate failure modes.

Recommended starting configuration, to be benchmark-tuned:

```yaml
routing:
  local_queue_wait_seconds: 120
  local_connection_timeout_seconds: 15
  local_first_token_timeout_seconds: 300
  local_total_request_timeout_seconds: 1200
  cloud_fallback_on_queue_timeout: true
  cloud_fallback_on_slow_ttft_alone: false
  cloud_fallback_on_connection_failure: true
  cloud_fallback_on_local_5xx: policy_controlled
```

The values are intentionally generous because the objective is to use local inference when it is functioning, not optimize for minimum latency at any cost.

Per-model values should replace these defaults once sufficient history exists.

### 10.3 Capacity-aware admission

Every deployment stores a qualification envelope:

```text
qualified_max_context_per_request
qualified_concurrency
qualified_aggregate_context
recommended_concurrency
hard_concurrency_limit
KV cache dtype
GPU memory utilization target
preemptio

*(truncated, 52331 chars total)*

**What the overnight run delivered:** OpenRouter fallback live (GLM 5.3 Flash), pilot CT116 built + static IP, GPU-power spend accounting live, reasoning-parser fleet rollout, benchmark engine + qualification envelopes, admission control (rpm caps), VM-level control ops (PVE token), CT906 pinned, RDMA-0 inventory complete.

## Spend accounting design (Jordan-approved 2026-09-10)
- Energy attributed from **GPU power telemetry only** (`gpu_samples`); server-total mode = GPU watts + platform estimate (idle_non_gpu=120 W/host, cpu 0.35 W/% — assumptions, in manager_settings)
- $/1M tokens = LiteLLM daily per-key tokens vs attributed kWh same window; rolling 7d/30d; degrades gracefully (gpu_samples start 2026-09-09)
- Rate: componentized `electricity_rates` — winter 15.82¢ (Sep–May = .09992 + .05830), summer 19.23¢ (Jun–Aug = .13398 + .05830)
- **No PDU polling by design** — PDUs stay free for control traffic

## Key pitfalls (learned, don't relearn)
- LiteLLM block payload: singular `key` — plural `keys` silently no-ops.
- `ssh_run.py` empty-drain: scripted SSH returning nothing ≠ auth failure; use `noderun.py`.
- Qwen3.6 tool calls are XML-style; `hermes` parser silently fails (raw tags in content) — `qwen3_xml` is correct.
- GLM 5.3 Flash is a reasoning model: empty content + `finish_reason: length` under small token caps is expected, not an error.
- .110 = MIAM-00110 (offline ≠ free). Fail closed on IP ambiguity: corosync map + Kea CSV + ping/arping all required.
- .162 Mamba-cache ceiling not reached even at C48 (773 tok/s) — 64-seq cap protects; envelope conservative.
- Secrets: only in root-readable files on VM114/CT906; never in logs/git/chat.

### [2026-09-10T09:55:22.210638+00:00] assistant

Found the first real clue: all three services are active and the app answers 200 on localhost:8001 — but **nginx has no listener on :443 at all** (curl to it returns 000), and the app binds only to 127.0.0.1:8001. That explains all three refusals from your laptop: nginx (the front door for both the DNS name and :443) isn't listening externally, and :8001 is loopback-only by design. Disk is fine (8%). Digging into why nginx dropped 443:

### [2026-09-10T09:55:27.491409+00:00] assistant

Root cause confirmed: **the nginx site config only ever had a port-80 listener** (`listen 80 default_server`) — no TLS block exists at all. That predates today (config last touched Sep 9 14:31, journal shows only clean reloads), so this isn't an outage from the overnight run; HTTPS on VM114 was never actually implemented — the plan requires it. Two fixes needed: add a proper 443 TLS server block, and bind :8001/:4000 stays loopback by design (nginx proxies). Checking what TLS material already exists before writing the config:

Cross-check THREE sources: (1) `/etc/pve/corosync.conf` ring0_addr list = the definitive node mgmt IP map (nodes own whole number bands like .110-.119, .133-.135, .143-.149), (2) Kea reservation CSV (`/api/kea/dhcpv4/download_reservations` — md5-verified CSV, OPNsense web-session API), (3) ping+`arping -c2 -I vmbr0`. The user corrected a near-miss: MIAM-00110 was OFFLINE so .110 pinged dead but belongs to the node (user: \"10.0.20.110 is definitely used as a static IP for MIAM-00110\"). DHCP pool .190-.250 is off-limits for statics; pick the lowest free gap (e.g. .187), set it via container netplan, then add a Kea reservation (`/api/kea/dhcpv4/add_reservation/` with `{reservation: {subnet: <uuid>, ip_address, hw_address, hostname, description}}` — subnet uuid from `search_reservation/` rows) so the pool can't lease it later.\n- **OPNsense scripted access (no SSH, no API keys):** web-session login works — POST /index.php with ALL hidden CSRF inputs + `usernamefld`/`passwordfld` + `login=1`, then `X-CSRFToken` header (regex from any /ui page JS: `setRequestHeader\\(\"X-CSRFToken\",\\s*\"([^\"]+)\"`) for `/api/...` MVC calls. Kea lease search: `/api/kea/leases4/search/`; reservations: `/api/kea/dhcpv4/search_reservation/` / `add_reservation/`. Login page must be fetched from a vantage that can reach 10.0.10.1:80 (the Proxmox nodes can; CT906 cannot). Full recipe: proxmox skill `references/opnsense-unbound-dns.md`.\n\n## User preferences embedded (2026-09)\n\n- **\"Fix what you create; don't leave broken artifacts\"** — when the user asks to review\n  or fix problems, they expect actual repairs + verification, not a findings report\n  (\"fix any problems that you've created\"). Prefer static IPs for infrastructure roles,\n  but if an address choice is uncertain, **fall back to DHCP rather than risk a\n  collision** (\"Use DHCP if you are confused instead of static IP assignment\") —\n  ambiguity on IP assignment is the one case where DHCP is the user-endorsed default.\n- **Write-functional core first** — monitoring-only dashboards disappoint; keys + completions + model control are the deliverable (see Golden rule).\n- **Scope, don't drop, deferred work** — the user explicitly approves working through all plan stages in one continuous run; anything genuinely deferred (e.g. 100 Gb RDMA ring: \"do this last, scope it for future work\") goes into the handoff doc as an explicit scoped section, never silently omitted.\n- **Name resources after their consumer** — LDAP groups/service accounts carry the VM/hostname (e.g. `llm-manager-vm114-admin`, `svc-llm-manager114`), not generic role names.\n- **Power telemetry sources (Jordan rule, 2026-09-10):** GPU NVML = the primary dynamic signal; Emporia = independent facility/whole-circuit view; **PDUs are reserved for control — never poll PDU network endpoints for power telemetry** (\"we do not want to block the PDUs with network traffic\"; at most a once-daily spot read). Local-model $/token accounting: GPU watts × effective seasonal electricity rate (componentized records), rolling 7/30-day windows, reported in both GPU-attributed (primary basis) and server-total (high-end margin) variants.\n- **\"Continue until finished; if you hit a roadblock, work on other related tasks\"** — when the user authorizes full-plan execution, keep every phase on the todo list, dispatch independent workstreams in parallel (delegate_task), and never end a turn with only a plan or a partial report. Rule: when a password hash, TLS cert, or sealed-secret step shows up in output, add one plain-language line (\"this is a login check, not storage encryption; nothing on disk is encrypted; no performance impact\"). Never let crypto vocabulary pass unexplained — the user reads it as hidden storage-encryption scope creep.\n\n## Web control plane: auth + role mapping (built 2026-09-09)\n\n- **Login session flow**: FastAPI `SessionMiddleware` (key in a 600 secret file) + LDAP\n  bind-login via the service bind account, then verify the user by binding AS the user DN.\n  Role = strongest `llm-manager-vm114-*` group. Write APIs gated on role sets\n  (Operator→restarts; Admin/Security-Admin→key issuance + unit edits).\n- **nginx route table must enumerate EVERY route the app serves** — `/login`, `/logout`,\n  `/admin`, `/api/`, `/healthz` — anything unlisted falls into `location / → 302 /admin` and\n  produces an infinite redirect loop (browser: ERR_TOO_MANY_REDIRECTS; tests: 302 chains).\n  After adding app routes, `nginx -t && systemctl reload nginx`.\n- **LiteLLM admin API verbs**: `/key/generate`, `/key/block`, `/key/delete`, `/key/update`\n  are POST, but **`/key/list` AND `/key/info` are GET** (POST returns 405 Method Not Allowed).\n  - `GET /key/list` returns `{\"keys\": [<raw token STRINGS>], \"total_count\": …}` — NOT\n    objects. Iterating with `k.get(...)` throws `'str' object has no attribute 'get'`.\n    Fetch per-key metadata with `GET /key/info?key=<token>` (query param, not JSON body);\n    the useful fields live under `info` (`key_alias`, `spend`, `max_budget`, `blocked`,\n    `expires`, `models`).\n  - Truncated 502 responses from `/key/generate` usually mean LiteLLM can't reach its Postgres — check\n  the litellm journal for `httpx.ConnectError` before touching app code. Then diagnose the\n  unreachable-DB in order: (1) port REFUSED while ICMP/SSH work + service verified\n  listening on the DB CT = **IP collision with a physical host** (compare `ip neigh <ip>`\n  MAC vs the CT's real NIC MAC — this happened: a DB CT sat on a cluster node's corosync\n  address; see proxmox skill's network audit reference), (2) pg_hba/grants only after\n  connectivity is proven.\n- **DB relocation runbook** (moved CT across IPs): grep every consumer for the old IP\n  (`grep -rn \"10.0.20.<old>\" /opt/<app>/ /etc/<app>/`), sed-repoint app `PG_HOST` and\n  gateway `database_url` in the same pass, `systemctl restart` BOTH services, then prove\n  with the service ports (app login page + gateway `/v1/models` 401-with-bad-key = healthy\n  upstream), not just `systemctl is-active`. An unrepointed stale reference survived one\n  move and was only caught during a later network audit.\n- **Audit everything to a JSONL file** (`/var/log/llm-manager/audit.log`): login ok/denied +\n  reason, key issue/block/revoke, unit edits, restarts. The audit log is what makes\n  debugging auth failures possible (\"unknown user\" vs \"invalid credentials\" vs \"no role\"\n  each pinpoint a different broken layer).\n- **Guest control agent pattern** (no arbitrary shell): fixed-command script\n  `/opt/llm-control/llm-control.sh {status|get-unit|set-unit|restart|logs|nvidia}` per vLLM\n  guest; gateway host holds a dedicated SSH keypair (`/etc/llm-manager/secrets/keys/`),\n  pub key appended to each guest's `authorized_keys` via the owning node + base64\n  `qm guest exec` (stdin is NOT forwarded — see proxmox skill). Unit edits: validate\n  `[Service]` + `ExecStart=` present, keep `.bak.<ts>`, `daemon-reload` WITHOUT restart —\n  the UI instructs the operator to restart separately (save ≠ apply).\n- **Keep the relay path documented for remote administration.** ssh_run.py (root+pexpect)\n  reaches Proxmox NODES only; the gateway VM takes a different path: land script on\n  owning node → `scp ubuntu@<vm-ip>` → `ssh … \"sudo bash /tmp/x.sh\"`. A session burned a\n  round-trip trying to ssh_run.py straight into the VM (`Permission denied (publickey)`).\n  Before driving a guest, check which path applies (node = root/password, VM =\n  node-relay with the baked-in key).\n\n## Deployment recipes\n\n- **Never invent vLLM flags.** Recover the battle-tested serve command from the target guest's root `~/.bash_history` (last working variant) and encode it in a systemd unit. Environment: `HF_HOME`, `VLLM_USE_FLASHINFER_SAMPLER=0`, `VLLM_USE_DEEP_GEMM=0` are typical for this fleet.\n- **Per-deployment profiles.** The same logical model name can expose different `max_model_len` (observed 262144 vs 32768 on identical model IDs). Store concurrency/context envelopes per deployment; never assume homogeneity.\n- **Routable requires proof:** `/v1/models` returns the expected model AND a real chat completion succeeds — health endpoint alone is insufficient.\n- Qwen thinking models may emit reasoning inline unless `--reasoning-parser qwen3` is set — check parser flags when output contains \"Thinking Process...\". Thinking traces consume `max_tokens`: a \"max_tokens: 40\" test request can be fully consumed by the reasoning preamble before any answer text appears.\n- **Reasoning-parser fleet rollout pattern (verified 2026-09-10):** canary on ONE host first — add `--reasoning-parser qwen3` next to `--tool-call-parser qwen3_xml` in the unit, restart, verify `/v1/models` after the ~3-4 min reload, THEN confirm (a) `reasoning` field is separated and `content` is clean, (b) tool calling still works (parser combo must return proper `tool_calls`, not raw XML). Only then roll the rest, **staggered with per-host health gates** and automatic rollback if a host fails to come up in ~5 min. Unit NAMES differ per host (`vllm.service` vs `vllm-qwen.service`) — detect the RUNNING unit (`systemctl list-units --state=running | grep vllm`) instead of assuming; grep matching \"already set\" against the wrong unit file gives false positives. Clients get clean content via `\"chat_template_kwargs\": {\"enable_thinking\": false}` (e.g. `content: '27'` end-to-end through the gateway); with thinking ON, max_tokens is consumed by the reasoning preamble first.\n- **Hybrid-Mamba models (Qwen3.x family): `--max-num-seqs` must fit the Mamba cache.** Every concurrent sequence holds one Mamba cache block; 16 GB cards at `--gpu-memory-utilization 0.92` fit only ~92 blocks, so vLLM's default 256 aborts during CUDA-graph capture with `RuntimeError: max_num_seqs (256) exceeds available Mamba cache blocks (92)` and the unit crash-loops. Fix: `--max-num-seqs 64` (comfortable headroom, still generous for Hermes load). Never copy the 3080 hosts' turboquant KV/attention flags onto smaller-VRAM GPUs without qualification.\n- **Crash-loop signature:** systemd reports `active` while `NRestarts` climbs, worker PIDs change between checks, and nothing listens on :8000. Don't keep polling the port — pull `journalctl -u vllm --no-pager | grep -iE \"error|RuntimeError\"` for the root cause directly. First successful boot takes ~4–5 min (weights ≈40 s/rank + torch.compile); the compile cache makes later restarts much faster.\n- **Fleet unit management via node relay:** land scripts on the gateway VM → `scp -i /etc/llm-manager/secrets/keys/id_ed25519` to guests → `ssh root@<guest> \"bash /tmp/x.sh\"` (guests have the control key). Service names differ per host (`vllm.service` vs `vllm-qwen.service`) — always resolve the RUNNING unit dynamically before editing. `--help` on modern vLLM is truncated: use `vllm serve --help=<flag>` (e.g. `--help=reasoning-parser`) for a specific flag's existence.\n- **Re-registering a recovered backend into the gateway pool:** idempotent-edit `/etc/llm-manager/litellm_config.yaml` (skip if the endpoint IP is already present), `systemctl restart litellm`, then PROVE it: `GET /v1/models` with the master key AND one real chat completion through an agent virtual key (expect 200 + usage accounting).\n\n## Benchmarking + qualification envelopes (Phase 8/11, proven 2026-09-10)\n\n- **Streaming gives REAL TTFT** — non-streaming requests can't measure first-token latency; use `\"stream\": true` and timestamp the first `data:` chunk. Benchmark client: thread pool at preset concurrency (C1/C10/C16/C20/C32/C48), 100 requests (20 for C1), fixed pad prompt (~4096 tokens), `temperature: 0`, thinking-off.\n- **Sweep before setting envelopes.** The 5060 Ti host (.162, Mamba-cache-limited) passed C10→C48 with ZERO failures at 4096-in/256-out (354→773 tok/s) — its Mamba ceiling was never reached because `--max-num-seqs 64` protects it; set envelopes conservatively (rec 16 / hard 32) regardless. The 3080 hosts match the plan §3.1 baseline at C10 (254 vs 249.85 tok/s) — treat a C10 run ≥ baseline as validation, then test the hard-limit concurrency (C20) before stamping `hard_concurrency_limit`.\n- Store runs in PG (`benchmark_runs`: preset, concurrency, ok/failed, tok/s, TTFT avg/max, latency avg, target, notes) and the approved envelope in `qualification_envelopes` per deployment IP. Envelope → gateway enforcement v1: per-deployment `rpm:` limits in LiteLLM model_list params (rec_concurrency × ~30 req/min; e.g. 300 rpm for C10 hosts, 480 for the C16 host), then `systemctl restart litellm` and re-verify gateway 200 + one real completion.\n- **Known-good engine script**: `scripts/bench_engine.py` in this skill — self-contained, streaming TTFT, RESULT_JSON line for machine parsing. Copy per session; per-deployment `--deployment http://<ip>:8000` targets one backend directly (bypasses gateway round-robin).\n\n## Power-based spend accounting (proven live 2026-09-10, v0.5.0)\n\n- **Design (user-approved):** energy attributed from GPU NVML watts (primary) with a server-total mode adding per-host platform estimate (`idle_non_gpu_watts=120` + `0.35 W per CPU-util-point` — documented assumptions, refine vs Emporia later). Token counts from LiteLLM `LiteLLM_SpendLogs` (prompt_tokens/completion_tokens/api_key/startTime) — that table lives in the **litellm DB**, NOT the app DB. $/1M tokens = attributed USD ÷ (tokens/1e6), rolling 24h/7d/30d windows; telemetry history is short at first, degrade gracefully and label the data window.\n- **First real numbers** (50k tokens/24h): GPU-attributed ≈ $0.68/1M tokens, server-total ≈ $37/1M. Both modes displayed; GPU-attributed is the user-approved primary basis.\n- Implementation pattern for the single-file FastAPI app: additive patch script (backup `main.py.bak.<ts>` first, abort if already patched), insert routes before a known section marker, add a dashboard `<h2>` card + loader JS before the boot line, then TestClient with the hand-signed cookie BEFORE `systemctl restart`, and verify `/healthz` + `/login` 200 after. Componentized rate via the existing `effective_rate()` — never hardcode the tariff.\n\n## Bounded VM-level control via Proxmox API token (plan §12.1, proven 2026-09-10)\n\n- **Token setup** (on any cluster node): `pveum user add llm-manager@pve` → ACL `PVEVMUser` + `PVEAuditor` on `/vms/<vmid>` for the 4 inference VMs only → `pveum user token add llm-manager@pve manager-control`. **privsep=0** (with privsep=1 the token inherits only user∩token perms and status calls fail `Permission check failed (/vms/103, VM.Audit)` even with user ACLs in place).\n- Token goes to the gateway VM as a 600 root secret (`/etc/llm-manager/secrets/pve_token`); call header `Authorization: PVEAPIToken=llm-manager@pve!manager-control=<tok>`; API at `https://<any-node>:8006/api2/json` with `-k` (self-signed).\n- **VMs live on their OWN nodes** — `nodes/miam-00135/qemu/103` 404s with \"Configuration file does not exist\" even though node 135 answers; keep a fixed IP→(node,vmid) map (10.0.20.161→miam00111/103, .162→miam00112/401, .163→miam00143/109, .164→miam00144/111) and reject arbitrary input. `/status/current` nests everything under `data` (`status`, `name`, `uptime`, `mem`) — reading top-level keys yields nulls that look like permission failures.\n- Known-good allowlist-only helper: `scripts/pve_ops.py` (vm-status/start/shutdown/reboot; NO force-stop, NO qm set, NO host ops — plan §12.1/§23 fail-closed rules). Feed it to the recovery FSM: DEGRADED → restart vLLM ≤2×/15min

### [2026-09-10T09:57:27.671643+00:00] assistant

Found it and fixed it. Here's what happened:

## Root cause
**nginx had no HTTPS listener at all.** The manager's web config was port-80 only from day one — the "TLS-capable nginx" step was never actually implemented, so every `https://` URL returned ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED. And `:8001`/`:4000` are deliberately bound to localhost-only (by design, plan §23 — API keys must never travel over plain HTTP), so that URL would refuse from your laptop even when everything is healthy.

The services themselves were perfectly healthy the whole time — app answered 200 on localhost, disk fine, all services active. It was purely a missing front door.

## Fix applied & verified
- Generated a self-signed TLS cert (10-year, covers both `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal` and `10.0.20.108`) → `/etc/llm-manager/tls/`
- Added the 443 server block mirroring all routes (admin, API, /v1 gateway, login)
- Port 80 now redirects to HTTPS (except `/healthz`, kept plain for probes)
- Verified from three vantages: VM114 localhost ✓, CT116 (.187) → `302 → /login 200` ✓, CT906 (.195) → `200` ✓ (bonus: CT906 can actually reach the UI directly over 443 — the old firewall concern doesn't apply here)
- `/v1/models` correctly returns 401 without a key — auth gate intact

## What to do on your laptop (MIAM-00101)
Use either of these — **skip `:8001`, that one will still refuse by design** (it's loopback-only; everything goes through nginx):

1. `https://10.0.20.108/admin`
2. `https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/admin`

Expect a browser warning (**"Your connection is not private" / ERR_CERT_AUTHORITY_INVALID**) — that's the self-signed cert, not a malfunction. Click **Advanced → Proceed**. If the DNS name doesn't resolve from the Tailscale side, the IP form will work regardless.

## Housekeeping
- Old port-80-only config backed up to `/etc/nginx/backups/` on VM114
- Handoff doc updated with the TLS fix; both new pitfalls (no-443-listener signature, never park nginx backups in `sites-enabled/`) saved to the gateway skill

If you still get refused from the laptop after this, tell me — the next suspect would be an ACL between Tailscale and the LAN, but the server side is now proven good from three different sources.

Fixed customer charges excluded per plan §16.2.\")\n    return out\n\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------- Spend accounting (GPU-power-based $/token)\nSPEND_IDLE_NON_GPU_WATTS = 120.0   # per-host platform assumption (plan §15.2); refine vs Emporia later\nSPEND_CPU_W_PER_UTIL_PT = 0.35     # rough CPU-load component per util % point\n\n\n@app.get(\"/api/spend\")\ndef api_spend(request: Request, window: str = \"24h\"):\n    \"\"\"Per-key + fleet token/energy/cost rollup.\n\n    Energy attribution: fleet GPU watts (host_power_rollup_1m) over the window,\n    optionally plus per-host non-GPU platform estimate (server-total mode).\n    Token counts: LiteLLM_SpendLogs (litellm DB) in the same window.\n    \"\"\"\n    user, role = current_user(request)\n    if not user:\n        return JSONResponse({\"error\": \"auth required\"}, status_code=401)\n    if window not in (\"24h\", \"7d\", \"30d\"):\n        window = \"24h\"\n    hours = {\"24h\": 24, \"7d\": 168, \"30d\": 720}[window]\n    now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)\n    rate, rate_src = effective_rate(now.month)\n    out = {\"window\": window, \"rate\": {\"effective_per_kwh\": rate, \"source\": rate_src},\n           \"modes\": {}, \"keys\": [], \"fleet\": {}}\n    conn = _pg()\n    if not conn:\n        out[\"error\"] = \"postgres unavailable\"\n        return out\n    lit = None\n    try:\n        pw = _load_pg_pw()\n        lit = psycopg2.connect(host=PG_HOST, dbname=\"litellm\", user=PG_USER, password=pw,\n                               connect_timeout=4)\n        # ---- tokens per key over window ----\n        with lit.cursor() as lcur:\n            lcur.execute(\"\"\"\n                SELECT api_key,\n                       COUNT(*) AS reqs,\n                       COALESCE(SUM(prompt_tokens),0) AS pt,\n                       COALESCE(SUM(completion_tokens),0) AS ct\n                FROM \"LiteLLM_SpendLogs\"\n                WHERE \"startTime\" >= now() - (%s || ' hours')::interval\n                GROUP BY api_key ORDER BY ct DESC\"\"\", (str(hours),))\n            key_rows = lcur.fetchall()\n            total_pt = sum(r[2] for r in key_rows)\n            total_ct = sum(r[3] for r in key_rows)\n            total_reqs = sum(r[1] for r in key_rows)\n        # ---- energy over same window (GPU-measured) ----\n        with conn.cursor() as cur:\n            cur.execute(\"\"\"\n                SELECT COALESCE(SUM(gpu_watts_avg),0)/60.0/1000.0\n                FROM host_power_rollup_1m\n                WHERE ts >= now() - (%s || ' hours')::interval\n                  AND ts < date_trunc('minute', now())\"\"\", (str(hours),))\n            gpu_kwh = float(cur.fetchone()[0] or 0.0)\n            # per-host avg gpu watts + cpu util for server-total estimate\n            cur.execute(\"\"\"\n                SELECT r.host_id, AVG(r.gpu_watts_avg),\n                       COALESCE(AVG(g.util_pct),0)\n                FROM host_power_rollup_1m r\n                LEFT JOIN gpu_samples g ON g.host_id = r.host_id\n                     AND g.ts >= now() - (%s || ' hours')::interval\n                WHERE r.ts >= now() - (%s || ' hours')::interval\n                GROUP BY r.host_id\"\"\", (str(hours), str(hours)))\n            host_rows = cur.fetchall()\n        n_hosts = len(host_rows) or 4\n        non_gpu_w = SPEND_IDLE_NON_GPU_WATTS * n_hosts\n        cpu_w = sum(float(r[2] or 0) for r in host_rows) * SPEND_CPU_W_PER_UTIL_PT\n        server_kw = (gpu_kwh * 1000.0 + non_gpu_w * hours + cpu_w * n_hosts * hours) / 1000.0\n        # server-total watts time = (gpu_kwh*1000 + non_gpu_w*h + cpu_avg_w*n*h)/1000\n        # (cpu util averaged across all gpu samples per host; approximation documented)\n        out[\"modes\"] = {\n            \"gpu_attributed\": {\"kwh\": round(gpu_kwh, 3), \"usd\": round(gpu_kwh * rate, 4)},\n            \"server_total\": {\"kwh\": round(server_kw, 3), \"usd\": round(server_kw * rate, 4)},\n        }\n        # ---- $/1M tokens (both modes) ----\n        for mode, kwh in ((\"gpu_attributed\", gpu_kwh), (\"server_total\", server_kw)):\n            om = out[\"modes\"][mode]\n            denom = (total_pt + total_ct) / 1e6\n            om[\"cost_per_1m_tokens_usd\"] = round(om[\"usd\"] / ((total_pt + total_ct) / 1e6), 4) if denom else None\n            out_tok = total_ct / 1e6\n            om[\"usd_per_1m_output_tokens\"] = round(om[\"usd\"] / out_tok, 4) if out_tok else None\n        # ---- per-key breakdown ----\n        for khash, reqs, pt, ct in key_rows:\n            share = ((pt + ct) / (total_pt + total_ct)) if (total_pt + total_ct) else 0\n            out[\"keys\"].append({\n                \"api_key\": (khash[:8] + \"…\") if isinstance(khash, str) and len(khash) > 12 else khash,\n                \"requests\": reqs, \"prompt_tokens\": pt, \"completion_tokens\": ct,\n                \"attributed_gpu_kwh\": round(gpu_kwh * share, 4),\n                \"attributed_cost_usd\": round(gpu_kwh * rate * share, 4),\n                \"cost_per_1m_tokens_usd\": round((gpu_kwh * rate * share) / ((pt + ct) / 1e6), 4) if (pt + ct) else None,\n            })\n        out[\"fleet\"] = {\"requests\": total_reqs, \"prompt_tokens\": total_pt,\n                        \"completion_tokens\": total_ct, \"total_tokens\": total_pt + total_ct}\n    except Exception as e:\n        out[\"error\"] = f\"spend rollup failed: {e.__class__.__name__}: {e}\"\n    finally:\n        try:\n            conn.close()\n        except Exception:\n            pass\n        if lit:\n            try:\n                lit.close()\n            except Exception:\n                pass\n    out[\"note\"] = (\"GPU-attributed energy is measured (NVML); server-total adds per-host \"\n                   f\"platform estimate (idle {SPEND_IDLE_NON_GPU_WATTS}W + CPU \"\n                   f\"{SPEND_CPU_W_PER_UTIL_PT} W/util-pt). Token/energy windows align; \"\n                   \"telemetry history starts 2026-09-09, shorter windows may be partial.\")\n    return out\n\n\n@app.get(\"/api/spend/summary\")\ndef api_spend_summary(request: Request):\n    \"\"\"Compact card data for the dashboard.\"\"\"\n    user, role = current_user(request)\n    if not user:\n        return JSONResponse({\"error\": \"auth required\"}, status_code=401)\n    s = api_spend(request, \"24h\")\n    if s.get(\"error\"):\n        return {\"error\": s[\"error\"]}\n    rate, _ = effective_rate(datetime.now(timezone.utc).month)\n    g = s[\"modes\"][\"gpu_attributed\"]\n    st = s[\"modes\"][\"server_total\"]\n    return {\n        \"tokens_24h\": s[\"fleet\"].get(\"total_tokens\", 0),\n        \"requests_24h\": s[\"fleet\"].get(\"requests\", 0),\n        \"energy_kwh_24h\": g[\"kwh\"],\n        \"cost_usd_24h\": g[\"usd\"],\n        \"cost_usd_30d_server_total\": None,  # filled by /api/spend 30d when needed\n        \"per_1m_gpu\": g.get(\"usd_per_1m_tokens_gpu\") or g.get(\"cost_per_1m_tokens_usd\"),\n        \"per_1m_server_total\": st.get(\"usd_per_1m_output_tokens\") or st.get(\"cost_per_1m_tokens_usd\"),\n        \"effective_rate_per_kwh\": rate,\n        \"note\": s.get(\"note\"),\n    }\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------- OpenRouter fallback config (Phase 12 pre-work)\n\ndef _setting(key, default=None):\n    conn = _pg()\n    if not conn:\n        return default\n    try:\n        with conn.cursor() as cur:\n            cur.execute(\"SELECT value FROM manager_settings WHERE key = %s\", (key,))", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

### [2026-09-10T11:03:07.553458+00:00] assistant

Everything from this batch is closed out — I finished both timed-out agents' work inline. Final report:

## ✅ RDMA-1 qualification — COMPLETE (the headline result)

| Test | Result |
|---|---|
| **RDMA verbs (RC/RoCEv2)** | **11,619 MB/s forward + 11,644–11,684 MB/s reverse ≈ 93–94 Gbit/s both directions** — near wire speed |
| iperf3 TCP (4 streams) | 35.8 / 36.7 Gbit/s — capped by the PCIe x8-downgraded slots, **not** the fabric |
| Key insight for multi-node vLLM | NCCL uses RDMA verbs, not TCP — so collectives should run at ~93 Gbit/s despite the TCP numbers |

Getting there required resolving RoCEv2 GID-type mismatches (both sides pinned to GID index 3 — each port's RoCEv2 index differs) and a perftest syntax quirk (`-x N`, not `--gid_index`; `-c` = connection type). Recorded in `RDMA1-RESULTS.md` and the gateway skill.

**Ring state in the manager UI:** MIAM-00111 and MIAM-00144 now show **qualified** with RDMA-1 evidence stored (JSONB), satisfying two of three §19.8 participants. MIAM-00143 remains unqualified — its ports link at 1 Gb, needs a physical cabling look.

## ✅ Emporia subagent's work — already superseded
I completed the adapter inline earlier while it was stuck (its 600s window expired with pyemvue installed but nothing verified). Current state stands: collector service live on VM114, compute/cooling/other role mapping active, facility rows rendering in Power & Cost.

## 📊 Program status after today

| Workstream | Status |
|---|---|
| Ollama on MIAM-00149 (`startupteams/ollama`) | ✅ E2E through gateway |
| Emporia Phase 6 (facility + cooling attribution) | ✅ Live |
| Spend & Efficiency (OpenRouter-style) | ✅ Live |
| Distributed/RDMA ring UI + §19.8 gate | ✅ Live |
| RDMA-1 (111↔144) | ✅ Qualified |
| TLS front door | ✅ Fixed (that was this morning's outage) |

**Next gates** (tracked in handoff): RDMA-2 — prove NCCL `NET/IB/GDRDMA` from inside the inference guests, which first needs the CX5 passthrough/SR-IOV design decision; 143's cabling inspection; and the bounded recovery FSM. Say the word on any of them.

### [2026-09-10T14:48:15.061963+00:00] assistant

**Found the issue.** The SFP/QSFP module IDs explain everything:

- **143's two ports + 111's f0 port** have transceiver codes `0x00` (blank type) — these are the cheap OEM 2m DACs that negotiate **1 Gb only**
- **144's f0** (the proven 100 Gb link) reads `0x80: 100G Base-CR4 / 25G Base-CR CA-L` — a genuine 100 G-capable DAC

So the ring cabling is physically fine, but the OEM 2m DACs on **111-f0↔143-f0** and **143-f1↔144-f1** are 1 G-limited parts. Since you say all three are 100 G-intended and cabling looks right, trying to force 25G/100G mode on the 143 pair — if the DACs genuinely can't, we'll know definitively:

### [2026-09-10T15:27:02.539554+00:00] assistant

Ring work advanced substantially. Final report on this session's push:

## ✅ RDMA-2 COMPLETE — NCCL works between the inference VMs

**CX5 passthrough executed** (staggered, both verified):
- MIAM-00111 → port 04:00.1 into **VM103**; MIAM-00144 → port 04:00.0 into **VM111** (each CX5 was alone in its IOMMU group → clean 1:1 VFIO, no ACS workarounds)
- Inside the guests: **100 Gb negotiated**, rdma-core + perftest installed, in-guest **verbs RC = 11,006 MB/s (~88.5 Gbit/s)** guest-to-guest
- **The big one: 2-node NCCL test PASSED** — torchrun across VM103↔VM111, all_reduce returned `ALLREDUCE_OK True` over **NET/IB RC QPs (RoCEv2)** — genuine RDMA transport, sockets never used

**One honest caveat, fully documented:** NCCL reports `GDR 0` — GPUDirect data path isn't active. Root cause found: `nvidia-peermem` refuses to load (`EINVAL`) because the guests run **Secure Boot with lockdown=integrity** and the DKMS peermem module is unsigned. Options: (a) MOK-sign the module, (b) disable Secure Boot in those VMs, or (c) proceed on staged RDMA — which still delivers ~88.5 Gbit/s and full multi-node capability. This is your call; I did not fabricate a "GDRDMA proven" result — NET/IB is proven, GDR is documented as blocked with the fix path.

**Fleet status:** all 4 vLLM hosts back to 200 and serving; one E2E gateway completion verified after the restarts.

## 🔍 143 cabling verdict (from your confirmation that it's all connected)

The cabling **is** good — but the QSFP EEPROMs revealed the actual problem: the DACs on **111-f0↔143-f0** and **143-f1↔144-f1** are OEM **1G-only** parts (blank transceiver codes, unequalized 2 m copper), while the working 111-f1↔144-f0 leg uses a genuine 100G-CR4-rated DAC. I force-tested 100G and 25G on 143 (link drops — the modules physically can't clock it), then restored everything to 1 Gb UP. **Two DAC swaps** complete the 3-node ring; full audit in `RDMA-RING-DAC-AUDIT-20260910.md`.

## Where the RDMA program stands

| Phase | Status |
|---|---|
| RDMA-0 inventory | ✅ |
| RDMA-1 link qualification (111↔144) | ✅ 93–94 Gbit/s verbs |
| RDMA-2 guest exposure + NCCL | ✅ NET/IB proven (GDR optional, documented) |
| RDMA-3 multi-node vLLM small model | ⏭️ next — both guests are NCCL-ready now |
| 143 activation | 🛒 needs 2× 100G-CR4 DACs |
| §19.8 gate | 111 + 144 qualified in the manager's ring registry |

**Natural next step:** RDMA-3 — spin the multi-node config (the generator in the Distributed card) for a small model across VM103+VM111. Want me to proceed with that (it'll take a maintenance window on those two hosts), and do you want GDR via Secure Boot change or staged RDMA for now?

# MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager — Plan vs. Implementation Status
**Date:** 2026-09-09 · **Reference:** `MARION-IA-USA_LLM_Manager_Implementation_Plan_v0.2.1.md`
**Question answered here:** *Have we implemented every part of the original plan? What remains?*

**Short answer: No — we have completed the core control-plane (roughly Phases 0–4, 7, 9, 10 partially, plus two unplanned repairs), but the majority of the operational/telemetry/qualification workstream (Phases 5–6, 8, 11–16) is still open.**

---

## 1. Completed (verified working as of 2026-09-09)

### Phase 0 — Read-only preflight ✅ (done, informal)
- [x] Cluster/node/guest inventory (PDM API); VMID + IP conflict checks
- [x] VM107/900/CT configs inspected; storage identified (no formal report format, but PREFLIGHT_REPORT.md exists)

### Phase 2 — Application shell + LDAP + audit ✅ (v0.3.0)

## 2. Remaining — by plan phase

- [ ] Recovery state machine: DEGRADED → restart vLLM ≤2×/15 min → VM reboot ≤1×/30 min → FAILED, all audited
- [ ] Explicit deployment states (UNKNOWN/HEALTHY/DEGRADED/DRAINING/RECOVERING/FAILED…)

### 🟢 Lower priority / later phases

**Phase 13–14 — 100 Gb ring + RDMA + multi-node vLLM** (plan §19)
- [ ] RDMA-0: read-only inventory of ConnectX-5 NICs on .111/.143/.144 (BDFs, firmware, MTU, peers, GPU-NIC topology)
- [ ] RDMA-1: per-link iperf3/verbs qualification (ring is 111↔143↔144↔111; .112 excluded until a 100 Gb path exists)
- [ ] RDMA-2: NCCL transport proof (want `NET/IB/GDRDMA`; sockets ≠ RDMA — must be shown *from inside the inference VMs*, which may need NIC passthrough/SR-IOV design first)
- [ ] RDMA-3/4: multi-node vLLM functional test → >120 GB model qualification → production routing gate (§19.8)
- Note: asset sheet shows the ring cabling exists (111 top-port↔143-bottom, 143 top↔144 bottom, 144 top↔111 bottom)

**Phase 15 — Storage expansion / full prompt-response logs**
- [ ] Design retains opt-in-only full logging (currently metadata-first ✅ per plan)
- [ ] Bulk-log storage on MIAM-00147/MIAM-00100 when needed; retention tiers (raw 14–30 d, 1-min agg 12 mo, aggregates long)

**Phase 16 — Advanced provider/policy engine**
- [ ] Direct provider integrations + cost/quality/policy scoring (official APIs only)

### 🧹 Operational hygiene (small, do with next work session)
- [ ] Pin CT906 (.195) with a static DHCP mapping on OPNsense (it leases inside the .190–.250 pool)
- [ ] PostgreSQL schema: plan §17.2 defines ~30 tables (hosts, gpus, deployments, gpu_samples, request_usage, benchmark_runs…); current DB has only audit + auth-adjacent tables — the telemetry schema is unbuilt
- [ ] Consider `--reasoning-parser qwen3` on vLLM units (model currently emits thinking text in content — noted in handoff; Hermes must budget max_tokens)

# MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager — Implementation Plan v0.2.1

**Status:** Design decisions incorporated; electricity-cost composition corrected; ready for read-only infrastructure preflight before deployment scripting  
**Decision date:** 2026-09-08  
**Target Proxmox host:** `MIAM-00135`  
**Candidate VMID:** `108` — **must be proven unused before creation**  
**Candidate IP:** `10.0.20.108` — **must be proven unused before assignment**  
**DNS name:** `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal`  
**Primary purpose:** Central control plane, OpenAI-compatible API gateway, Hermes-agent key authority, routing layer, benchmarking system, observability system, and cost/performance database for the MARION-IA-USA local LLM infrastructure.

---

## 1. Executive objective

Create a dedicated VM named:

```text
MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager
```

on `MIAM-00135`, initially bootstrapped from the known-good GPU Driver Installer controller VM architecture but fully re-identified and stripped of copied GDI secrets/state.

The LLM Manager will become the stable point through which local Hermes agents and other authorized internal clients use LLM inference. It should:

- monitor and control vLLM deployments on `MIAM-00111`, `MIAM-00112`, `MIAM-00143`, and `MIAM-00144`
- issue a distinct API key to each Hermes agent
- expose a stable OpenAI-compatible endpoint
- route requests to healthy local deployments first
- wait a deliberate, configurable interval for local capacity instead of prematurely buying cloud inference
- fall back to OpenRouter only when policy permits and local capacity/health criteria are not satisfied
- enforce OpenRouter spend caps and per-agent policy
- benchmark model/topology combinations and keep historical results
- collect GPU, vLLM, request, health, and power telemetry
- calculate energy cost and performance-per-dollar
- use GPU onboard telemetry as the primary dynamic local power signal
- ingest Emporia whole-circuit/PDU/mini-split data as an independent facility-level measurement source
- store operational metadata and history in PostgreSQL
- offload high-volume or full prompt/response history away from the constrained `MIAM-00135` system disk
- provide controlled vLLM service lifecycle actions and bounded VM recovery actions
- provide a future path for multi-node vLLM deployments using the 100 Gb interconnect between `MIAM-00111`, `MIAM-00143`, and `MIAM-00144`
- eventually compare/rank local inference, OpenRouter, direct APIs, and other officially supported provider integrations by availability, quality, cost, performance, context, privacy, and policy

The LLM Manager is a **control plane and gateway**. It does not normally host model weights or perform GPU inference itself.

---

## 2. Decisions locked in v0.2

The following answers from Section 28 of v0.1 are now treated as design decisions.

| Item | v0.2 decision |
|---|---|
| VM identity | Candidate `VMID 108`, candidate `10.0.20.108`; fail closed unless both are proven unused |
| Gateway | LiteLLM Proxy plus a Startup Teams custom management/control web application |
| Client keys | One distinct API key per Hermes agent |
| Qwen 3.6 normal load | Design around about 10 concurrent agents/requests, with an initial hard concurrency envelope of about 20 pending benchmark qualification |
| Routing default | Local first, deliberate wait/queue, OpenRouter fallback only if the key policy allows it |
| Cloud policy | Default OpenRouter master/aggregate budget must have hard caps; individual keys may override fallback permissions within administrator limits |
| VM control | Manager may control both vLLM service lifecycle and bounded inference-VM recovery/restart |
| Power | GPU telemetry first; modeled CPU/RAM/platform overhead; Emporia used as independent whole-circuit/facility telemetry |
| Electricity planning rate | Rate 400 base energy (`$0.09992/kWh` Sep-May; `$0.13398/kWh` Jun-Aug) + year-round Emporia-observed variable fees/riders adder of `$0.05830/kWh`; effective planning rates `$0.15822/kWh` Sep-May and `$0.19228/kWh` Jun-Aug (displayed as 15.82¢ and 19.23¢) |
| Logging | Metadata-first initially; architecture must support full prompt/response logs later with off-host storage |
| Admin auth | Existing LDAP/LLDAP identity system; local break-glass/bootstrap admin only if needed |
| DNS | `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal` |
| Database | PostgreSQL; application designed so PostgreSQL can reside on a future storage-oriented VM on `MIAM-00147` |
| Fallback timing | Local requests are given meaningful time to queue/run; slow local TTFT alone is not a reason to immediately duplicate the request in OpenRouter |
| Distributed inference | 100 Gb multi-node path is a first-class future capability but remains unroutable until independently qualified |

### Important interpretation: API keys versus concurrency

The number of issued API keys should **not** be limited to 20. API keys identify clients; concurrency consumes model capacity.

For the known `Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ` deployment, start with a deployment profile such as:

```yaml
recommended_concurrency: 10
initial_hard_concurrency_limit: 20
max_issued_agent_keys: unlimited_by_model_capacity
```

The concurrency values must be model/deployment specific. A different model, quantization, topology, context target, or GPU set may receive a different admission envelope.

---

## 3. Known inference baseline

### 3.1 MIAM-00111 qualified baseline

Known qualified environment:

```text
Host:             MIAM-00111
Proxmox:          VE 9.2.10
Inference VM:     VM103 jordatech-vllm-test
Guest:            Ubuntu 24.04.4 LTS
Kernel:           6.8.0-139-generic
GPUs:             6 x RTX 3080 20 GB
Aggregate VRAM:   120 GB
NVIDIA driver:    R580 open, module 580.173.02
Python env:       /opt/vllm-venv
API port:         8000
Model:            QuantTrio/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ
```

Working topology:

```text
TP1 / DP6 / EP6
```

Core flags:

```text
--tensor-parallel-size 1
--data-parallel-size 6
--enable-expert-parallel
--expert-placement-strategy linear
--quantization awq
--dtype half
--gpu-memory-utilization 0.92
--kv-cache-dtype turboquant_k8v4
--attention-backend TURBOQUANT
--enable-prefix-caching
--enable-chunked-prefill
--scheduler-reserve-full-isl
--watermark 0.05
--kv-cache-metrics
```

Validated large-context operating point:

```text
10 concurrent requests
229,376 input tokens per request
+ 256 output tokens per request
```

Do not record the synthetic 250k test as a qualified model limit because tokenizer expansion exceeded the declared 262,144-token model maximum.

Representative 4096-in/256-out throughput results:

| Concurrency | Output tok/s | Total tok/s | Mean TTFT |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 249.85 | 4259.49 | 4.27 s |
| 16 | 271.21 | 4623.65 | 4.73 s |
| 20 | 311.98 | 5318.69 | 5.20 s |
| 32 | 319.69 | 5450.14 | 7.39 s |
| 48 | 348.62 | 5943.41 | 11.13 s |

For normal Hermes service, concurrency 10 remains the initial preferred baseline even though aggregate throughput rises at higher concurrency.

### 3.2 MIAM-00112

Known major difference:

```text
6 x RTX 5060 Ti 16 GB
AMD Threadripper Pro 3955WX
128 GB RAM
```

Do not copy the MIAM-00111 topology without qualification. Its 96 GB aggregate VRAM and different GPU architecture require a separate model-fit and benchmark envelope.

### 3.3 MIAM-00143 and MIAM-00144

These are intended to reproduce or complement the MIAM-00111 inference stack, but each must independently inventory:

```text
GPU model/count/VRAM
PCI BDFs
IOMMU groups
kernel
NVIDIA driver/module packages
inference VM resources
100 Gb NICs/ports
network addresses
RDMA capability
GPU-to-NIC PCIe topology
```

Never copy PCI BDFs between hosts.

## 5. Base VM and bootstrap strategy

### 5.1 Candidate VM sizing

Start with:

```text
VMID:             108 candidate only
IP:               10.0.20.108 candidate only
Hostname:         marion-ia-usa-llm-manager
Display name:     MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager
OS:               Ubuntu Server 24.04 LTS
vCPU:             8
RAM:              16 GB
System disk:      64 GB
Local data disk:  64-128 GB only if actually available/needed
Machine:          q35
Firmware:         OVMF
QEMU agent:       enabled
Ballooning:       disabled
Autostart:        enabled after acceptance test
GPU passthrough:  none
```

Because `MIAM-00135` has constrained storage, do not assume a large local telemetry/database disk. Keep all high-volume paths configurable and relocatable.

### 5.2 Clone source

Use the existing controller:

```text
VM107
gpu-driver-installer-v4-1
10.0.20.107:8443
```

as a **bootstrap image only**.

Required clone hygiene:

1. Full-clone while source is in a consistent state.
2. Keep clone network-isolated until identity reset is complete.
3. Change VM display name and guest hostname.
4. Regenerate machine-id.
5. Regenerate SSH host keys.
6. Remove copied application secrets, private keys, audit records, state, tokens, and TLS identity.
7. Remove GDI application code/services not needed by LLM Manager.
8. Retain only useful base hardening, QEMU guest agent, service patterns, reverse-proxy/TLS patterns, and OS configuration.
9. Generate a new TLS certificate for `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal`.
10. Run a secret scan before enabling the new network interface.

### 5.3 Fail-closed VMID/IP preflight

Before any clone or network mutation, the deployment script must prove both identifiers are unused.

VMID validation should include at least:

```text
cluster-wide Proxmox resource lookup for VMID 108
MIAM-00135 qm list
/etc/pve configuration search
```

IP validation should include at least:

```text
existing Proxmox guest configuration search
DNS lookup
neighbor/ARP table lookup
ICMP probe
ARP duplicate-address detection from the correct VLAN
DHCP/static reservation inspection where available
```

A failed probe does **not** prove an IP is unused. Any contradictory or ambiguous signal aborts deployment.

---

## 6. Software stack

### 6.1 Gateway

Use **LiteLLM Proxy** as the provider-normalization and virtual-key gateway.

Reasons:

- OpenAI-compatible API surface
- virtual keys
- authentication/authorization hooks
- spend tracking and budgets
- rate limiting
- provider normalization
- retries/fallbacks
- local OpenAI-compatible/vLLM endpoints
- OpenRouter and future external provider adapters

The custom Startup Teams control application remains authoritative for infrastructure-specific logic such as vLLM lifecycle, GPU health, power, benchmark qualification, host maintenance, and MARION-specific routing eligibility.

### 6.2 Custom control application

Recommended:

```text
Python 3.12
FastAPI
Uvicorn/Gunicorn
SQLAlchemy
Alembic
PostgreSQL
systemd
Nginx or Caddy
```

Suggested layout:

```text
/opt/llm-manager/
    app/
    control/
    collectors/
    routing/
    benchmark/
    auth/
    power/
    providers/
    distributed/
    migrations/
    static/
    templates/

/etc/llm-manager/
    manager.yaml
    nodes.yaml
    models.yaml
    routes.yaml
    tls/
    secrets/

/var/lib/llm-manager/
    cache/
    benchmark-artifacts/
    exports/
    spool/

/var/log/llm-manager/
```

### 6.3 Service separation

Use separate systemd units where practical:

```text
llm-manager-web.service
llm-manager-collector.service
llm-manager-benchmark.service
llm-manager-control.service
litellm.service
```

Do not let a hung benchmark worker take down the gateway.

---

## 7. Network, DNS, TLS, and access

Expose one HTTPS name:

```text
https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/
```

Recommended routing:

```text
/admin      -> custom admin UI
/api        -> manager orchestration API
/v1         -> LiteLLM/OpenAI-compatible API gateway
/healthz    -> manager health
```

Access policy:

```text
LAN:             allowed
Tailscale:       allowed where policy permits
Public Internet: denied
```

Hermes clients should only require:

```bash
OPENAI_BASE_URL=https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/v1
OPENAI_API_KEY=<agent-specific-key>
OPENAI_MODEL=startupteams/general
```

They should never require:

- SSH access to inference servers
- Proxmox credentials
- vLLM host credentials
- OpenRouter master credentials
- direct provider master credentials

---

## 8. Admin authentication and authorization

The web UI should authenticate against the existing LDAP/LLDAP environment.

Recommended roles:

```text
LLM-Viewer
LLM-Operator
LLM-Admin
LLM-Security-Admin
```

Example permissions:

| Capability | Viewer | Operator | Admin | Security Admin |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| View status/metrics | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Run benchmark | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Drain/undrain | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Restart vLLM service | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Restart inference VM | No | Restricted | Yes | Yes |
| Edit deployment config | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| Create/rotate agent keys | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| Change provider master secrets | No | No | No | Yes |
| Change global spend caps | No | No | Yes | Yes |

A local bootstrap/break-glass administrator may exist for installation/recovery, but LDAP should be the routine administrative identity source.

**Secret handling:** do not hardcode or reproduce the root password, LDAP bind password, Emporia password, OpenRouter key, or LiteLLM master key in this plan, source control, shell history, or generated configuration. Inject them through root-readable secret files or a later secret manager.

---

## 9. Hermes API-key and policy model

### 9.1 One key per Hermes agent

Structure:

```text
Organization
  -> Project/service
      -> Hermes agent
          -> one or more rotating API credentials
```

Each stored key record should include:

```text
key_id
key_prefix
hash
agent_id
project_id
created_at
expires_at optional
enabled
allowed_logical_models
max_concurrency
requests_per_minute optional
tokens_per_minute optional
daily_token_budget optional
monthly_token_budget optional
daily_dollar_budget optional
monthly_dollar_budget optional
local_allowed
cloud_allowed
fallback_policy
provider_allowlist optional
logging_policy
```

Raw keys are shown only at creation/rotation.

### 9.2 Initial policy profiles

Support at least:

```text
LOCAL_ONLY
LOCAL_FIRST_OPENROUTER_FALLBACK
CLOUD_ALLOWED_EXPLICIT
SPECIFIC_MODEL_ONLY
COST_CAPPED
BENCHMARK_ONLY
```

Recommended default for normal Hermes agents:

```text
LOCAL_FIRST_OPENROUTER_FALLBACK
```

but only after OpenRouter global caps are configured.

### 9.3 OpenRouter budget controls

Before enabling fallback for any default/general key:

```text
[ ] global daily spend cap configured
[ ] global monthly spend cap configured
[ ] warning threshold configured
[ ] hard-stop threshold configured
[ ] per-project cap supported
[ ] per-agent cap supported
[ ] audit event generated on fallback
[ ] actual OpenRouter reported cost stored when available
```

Fallback should never silently convert an uncapped local workload into uncapped external spend.

---

## 10. Routing and admission policy

### 10.1 Default sequence

For a normal general-purpose Hermes request:

```text
1. Resolve logical model and agent policy.
2. Find healthy local deployments that satisfy context/capability constraints.
3. Apply deployment admission limits.
4. If local capacity is temporarily full, queue for the configured local patience interval.
5. If admitted locally, let the local request continue even if TTFT is slow.
6. Do not start a duplicate OpenRouter request merely because local TTFT is slower than expected.
7. If no local deployment becomes admissible before the queue deadline, and the key permits cloud fallback, use OpenRouter.
8. If local execution actually fails after dispatch and a retry is safe, apply the configured retry/fallback policy.
9. If cloud fallback is disallowed or budget-capped, return an explicit unavailable/capacity response.
```

### 10.2 Initial timeout model

Use separate timeouts for separate failure modes.

Recommended starting configuration, to be benchmark-tuned:

```yaml
routing:
  local_queue_wait_seconds: 120
  local_connection_timeout_seconds: 15
  local_first_token_timeout_seconds: 300
  local_total_request_timeout_seconds: 1200
  cloud_fallback_on_queue_timeout: true
  cloud_fallback_on_slow_ttft_alone: false
  cloud_fallback_on_connection_failure: true
  cloud_fallback_on_local_5xx: policy_controlled
```

The values are intentionally generous because the objective is to use local inference when it is functioning, not optimize for minimum latency at any cost.

Per-model values should replace these defaults once sufficient history exists.

### 10.3 Capacity-aware admission

Every deployment stores a qualification envelope:

```text
qualified_max_context_per_request
qualified_concurrency
qualified_aggregate_context
recommended_concurrency
hard_concurrency_limit
KV cache dtype
GPU memory utilization target
preemptio

*(truncated, 52331 chars total)*

- GLM 5.3 Flash is a reasoning model: empty content + `finish_reason: length` under small token caps is expected, not an error.

### [2026-09-10T16:11:19.117493+00:00] assistant

Found it and fixed it. Here's what happened:

## Root cause
**nginx had no HTTPS listener at all.** The manager's web config was port-80 only from day one — the "TLS-capable nginx" step was never actually implemented, so every `https://` URL returned ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED. And `:8001`/`:4000` are deliberately bound to localhost-only (by design, plan §23 — API keys must never travel over plain HTTP), so that URL would refuse from your laptop even when everything is healthy.

The services themselves were perfectly healthy the whole time — app answered 200 on localhost, disk fine, all services active. It was purely a missing front door.

## Fix applied & verified

Ring work advanced substantially. Final report on this session's push:

## ✅ RDMA-2 COMPLETE — NCCL works between the inference VMs

**CX5 passthrough executed** (staggered, both verified):
- MIAM-00111 → port 04:00.1 into **VM103**; MIAM-00144 → port 04:00.0 into **VM111** (each CX5 was alone in its IOMMU group → clean 1:1 VFIO, no ACS workarounds)
- Inside the guests: **100 Gb negotiated**, rdma-core + perftest installed, in-guest **verbs RC = 11,006 MB/s (~88.5 Gbit/s)** guest-to-guest
- **The big one: 2-node NCCL test PASSED** — torchrun across VM103↔VM111, all_reduce returned `ALLREDUCE_OK True` over **NET/IB RC QPs (RoCEv2)** — genuine RDMA transport, sockets never used

**One honest caveat, fully documented:** NCCL reports `GDR 0` — GPUDirect data path isn't active. Root cause found: `nvidia-peermem` refuses to load (`EINVAL`) because the guests run **Secure Boot with lockdown=integrity** and the DKMS peermem module is unsigned. Options: (a) MOK-sign the module, (b) disable Secure Boot in those VMs, or (c) proceed on staged RDMA — which still delivers ~88.5 Gbit/s and full multi-node capability. This is your call; I did not fabricate a "GDRDMA proven" result — NET/IB is proven, GDR is documented as blocked with the fix path.

**Fleet status:** all 4 vLLM hosts back to 200 and serving; one E2E gateway completion verified after the restarts.

## 🔍 143 cabling verdict (from your confirmation that it's all connected)

The cabling **is** good — but the QSFP EEPROMs revealed the actual problem: the DACs on **111-f0↔143-f0** and **143-f1↔144-f1** are OEM **1G-only** parts (blank transceiver codes, unequalized 2 m copper), while the working 111-f1↔144-f0 leg uses a genuine 100G-CR4-rated DAC. I force-tested 100G and 25G on 143 (link drops — the modules physically can't clock it), then restored everything to 1 Gb UP. **Two DAC swaps** complete the 3-node ring; full audit in `RDMA-RING-DAC-AUDIT-20260910.md`.
